### Capacity-Based Pricing

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/13872357395228-Capacity-Based-Pricing  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:33Z  
> Labels: 2BTr, edited_at

Platform pricing is flexible, scalable, and customizable, allowing customers to increase their plan capacity as business needs grow.

Phrase plans are based on usage. Usage is measured via the platform’s [pricing metrics](https://phrase.com/legal/?tab=platform-pricing-terms), with capacity limits applied to each plan per billing period. If an organization exceeds plan capacity, some application features may become restricted during the billing period until capacity is added or the plan is upgraded.

Platform [owners and administrators](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240 "Phrase User Management") can monitor capacity usage in the Subscription overview tab of the Organization settings page. Click through [tutorial](http://phrase.navattic.com/le80qaq) on monitoring usage.

#### Exceeding Capacity Limits

If an organization reaches or exceeds any pricing metric capacity, the feature that has reached its limit may become restricted across that organization. The [account administrator](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240 "Phrase User Management")(s) will be notified when an organization reaches the following capacity:

- ***When capacity has reached 80% of the plan limit.***

  At 80%, the organization will still have full access to all the plan's features. We encourage adding capacity or upgrading the plan to continue working uninterrupted.
- ***When capacity has reached 100% of the plan limit.***

  At 100%, the plan’s full capacity has been reached and the organization will be required to upgrade the plan or add capacity to continue working uninterrupted.
- ***When capacity is reached or exceeded.***

  Functionality and usage of features will be restricted. To regain full access, the organization will need to add capacity to the pricing metrics that have exceeded the capacity limits or upgrade the plan.

The following functionalities will be restricted when capacity is reached or exceeded:

- TMS Processed Words Capacity

  - Capacities are measured and consumed within one billing cycle and reset automatically at the end of each billing cycle.
  - Depletable capacities are adjusted for the billing cycle frequency.
  - Job imports:

    Importing jobs via the UI, API, or continuous localization features will create "ghost" jobs. These jobs will have no word count and cannot be downloaded. Jobs that have this status will need to be imported again after restrictions are removed.
  - Submitter Portal:

    Submitting projects through the Submitter Portal will be restricted.
- [MTU Capacity](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/11530492252444#UUID-3c8ba1b3-290a-5451-ba45-71797af5a755)

  - Capacities are measured and consumed within one billing cycle and reset automatically at the end of each billing cycle.
  - Depletable capacities are adjusted for the billing cycle frequency.
  - Machine translation:

    Will not be available in TMS analyses, pre-translation, and MT suggestions in the CAT editor.
- Strings Managed Words Capacity

  - Pricing Metrics with non-depletable capacities are not reset at the end of each billing cycle and can be increased at any point (or decreased).
  - Creating new projects

    Creating new projects through the UI and API will be restricted.
  - Updating existing keys

    Updating existing keys through the UI and API will be restricted.
  - Adding new keys

    Adding new keys and uploading files through the UI and API will be restricted.
  - Read-only access to all data will still be available as well as the ability to export languages and delete projects, languages, keys and jobs.
- Studio hours in [Phrase Studio](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/21177186715420#UUID-df5d31ff-16d9-6310-bc96-0818fca7a7d9)

  - Users will not be able to upload new content or localize and edit captions and translations for already uploaded videos.
- Orchestrator Workflows Capacity

  - Workflow publishing

    Workflow publishing will be restricted.
  - Executed Workflow Actions

    All workflows will be undeployed once the Actions capacity is reached. Executing workflows will not be possible after the subscription runs out of Actions capacity.
  - Active workflows

    Workflows running at the time of reaching the Actions limit will complete their execution.
- Custom AI Deployed Models

  - Custom AI deployed models

    Deploying further models will not be possible.

#### Pricing Metrics

There are three categories of Pricing Metrics:

1. Depletable

   Pricing Metrics with depletable capacities are measured and consumed within one billing cycle and reset automatically at the end of each billing cycle (annual or quarterly). Depletable capacities are adjusted for the billing cycle frequency.

   ***Example:***

   The Business Team plan has a default capacity of 2.5 million TMS Processed Words per year, if paid annually. If paid per quarter, TMS Processed Words allocated capacity per quarter would be 625,000.

   The following Pricing Metrics are Depletable:

   - [TMS Processed Words](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14409846225308#UUID-686ee438-7db4-5f6f-7f83-ff41950483a2)
   - MTUs
   - AI units
   - [Studio hours](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/22916939527196#UUID-6795c755-cd77-dd28-b435-2bb5d465da27) in Phrase Studio

   For customers who experience fluctuations in word count, we advise an annual billing cycle as it allows for more flexibility and management of the capacity limits.
2. Non-depletable

   Pricing Metrics with non-depletable capacities work similarly to the concept of storage. They DO NOT get reset at the end of each billing cycle and can be increased at any point in time (or decreased, as the case may be).

   ***Example:***

   An LSP Professional customer currently has 190,000 out of their 200,000 Strings Managed Words allocation stored in Phrase Strings. To create space for a large project, the customer deletes duplicate keys and old keys, reducing their stored Strings Managed Words to 170,000. A similar concept would apply to Strings Seats (a seat can be removed for one user and be given/allocated to another user.)

   The following Pricing Metrics are Non-depletable:

   - [Strings Managed Words](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/13381554813596#UUID-431263fa-b80f-34c7-d5ce-49c660dfef95)
   - Strings Seats
   - Orchestrator Workflows
   - Custom AI Deployed Models
   - Portal usage of Phrase Language AI and Phrase Portal
3. Secondary  metrics

   Secondary metrics are based on depletable metrics and have capacity limits based on your plan.

   The following are Secondary Pricing Metrics:

   - Orchestrator Actions

     An Orchestrator Workflow consists of a collection of individual automated processes (known as actions).
   - Custom AI Training Count

     The process of creating a new MT model or updating an existing one. Trainings are not limited to specific datasets.

   Details of the limits for Secondary metrics can be found on our [Pricing Page](https://phrase.com/pricing/#acc-limits) and in our [Terms of Service](https://phrase.com/legal/?tab=suite-pricing-terms).

#### Adding Capacity

Capacity can be added in-product (self-served) to the following Pricing Metrics for a [fee](https://phrase.com/pricing/#acc-capacity):

##### Note

Customers on Enterprise plans cannot add capacity in-product.

- TMS Processed Words
- Strings Managed Words
- Studio hours in Phrase Studio
- [Machine Translation Units (MTUs)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/11530492252444#UUID-3c8ba1b3-290a-5451-ba45-71797af5a755)

  There is one MTU pool for three capabilities:

  - Language AI in the CAT editor
  - Language AI over API
  - Phrase Portal
- Orchestrator Workflows
- Deployed Models (Custom AI)
- [AI Units (AIUs)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14032731809052#UUID-244d7872-8d03-3335-c502-dea9b1b12369 "AI Unit Calculation")

  There is one AIU pool for these capabilities:

  - Auto LQA
  - MT Optimize
  - Auto Adapt
  - AI Translation Agent

Capacity can be added to the following Pricing Metrics by speaking to your designated Customer Success Manager:

- Strings Seats
- Portal usage of Phrase Language AI and Phrase Portal

###### Affect on subscription costs

There are two types of added capacity:

| Recurring | One-time |
| --- | --- |
| This is a subscription-level recurring top-up. Capacity is added to the subscription at the time of the top-up and remains in place for future renewal(s). | This capacity is of a one-time nature. It does not increase the subscription level at the next subscription renewal. |
| We recommend this for customers whose usage is generally growing and is not influenced by spikes of usage due to one-time circumstances. It is also advisable for better cost forecasting. | We recommend this for customers who need more capacity unexpectedly, e.g. for a large one-off project. There is a premium surcharge for adding capacity this way (see below). |
| Any unused capacity is not rolled over to the next billing cycle. | Any unused capacity is not rolled over to the next billing cycle. |
| Recurring capacity can be added at any time (and multiple times) within a billing cycle. | One-time capacity can only be added once per billing cycle. |
| Recurring capacity top-ups benefit from price and time proration. The shorter the time period relevant for the added capacity (i.e., the closer the capacity is added to the subscription renewal), the lower the charge for the recurring capacity top-up. | One-time capacity top-ups do not benefit from time and price proration. They are added to the total capacity relevant to the billing cycle at the full unit price, irrespective of the time period remaining until the subscription renewal date. |
| Recurring capacity benefits from volume discounts (the more capacity purchased, the higher the expected discount). | Adding one-time capacity incurs a unit price premium of ~40%. They also do not benefit from any volume discounts. |
| Recurring capacity can be applied to Depletable and Non-depletable metrics. | One-time capacity can only be applied to Depletable metrics. |

Customers can add and pay for recurring and one-time capacity in-product for the relevant metrics.

### Credit-Based Pricing

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/30624146191260-Credit-Based-Pricing  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-28T06:18:55Z

On credit-based plans, the organization purchases a prepaid Phrase Credit Pool. Credits are deducted from the pool as Phrase capabilities are used. Each capability consumes credits at its own credit rate.

Binding definitions and calculation rules are set out in the [Phrase Platform Pricing Terms](https://phrase.com/legal/?tab=platform-pricing-terms).

##### View Credit Rates and Balance

Credit rates and the current credit balance are shown in the product. To view the credit rates, follow these steps:

1. From the Dashboard, navigate to Organization settings.
2. Select the Subscription overview tab.
3. The Subscription overview tab also shows credit consumption per month by capability, and a breakdown for the current billing period.

##### Credit Consumption by Capability

| Capability | Item | Measured by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Phrase TMS | Processed words | Words imported for translation, multiplied by the number of target languages |
| Phrase Strings | Managed words | Words stored, deducted daily while stored |
|  | Seats | Active seats, deducted daily |
| Phrase Studio | Studio minutes | Studio minutes: file duration for transcription, plus duration for each target language |
| Custom AI | Deployed models | Deployed models, deducted daily |
| Phrase Orchestrator | Workflows | Published workflows, deducted daily |
| Phrase Language AI | Portal active users | Monthly active users (MAUs), measured daily |
|  | Machine translation | Source characters sent to Phrase Language AI. The credit rate for machine translation depends on the type of engine used:  - Phrase-owned engines - 3rd party managed engines - BYO / manually managed engines - Other engines |
| Generative AI |  | Target words. See Generative AI Features below. |

##### Generative AI Features

Phrase calculates credit consumption for AI-powered (large language model, LLM) features from the number of target words submitted to Phrase. Each feature has its own credit rate.

The following features consume credits on this basis:

- [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9)
- [MT Optimize](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a)

  Recommended input limit for timely processing: 5,000 words.
- [Auto Adapt](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/18807673145372#UUID-13f11225-b03e-7da3-bc9f-1db0de86ea75)
- [Auto LQA](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14313477218588#UUID-be576442-c089-055d-5cba-4c0b50dea415)
- [AI Checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks")

  Only unlocked (evaluated) words are counted.

##### Note

For character-based target languages (Japanese, Korean, Simplified and Traditional Chinese, Thai), the number of characters is divided by two.

##### Credit Validity

Credits are valid for the whole subscription term, for example 12 months. This applies even when the subscription is invoiced in shorter intervals, such as quarterly. Credits left unused at the end of the subscription term expire and do not transfer to a renewed subscription.

All organizations under the subscription draw from the same Phrase Credit Pool, including staging, sandbox and test organizations.

##### Usage Beyond the Credit Pool

When the Phrase Credit Pool is used up, capabilities remain available. Further usage is charged as on-demand consumption, as described in the Pricing Terms.

##### Note

Organizations on capacity-based plans continue to use the pricing metrics described in [Capacity-Based Pricing](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/13872357395228#UUID-d0c73768-f31c-0510-0c3b-be5b46a76161 "Capacity-Based Pricing").

---

### AI Unit Calculation

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14032731809052-AI-Unit-Calculation  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-28T06:18:55Z  
> Labels: 2BTr, AI, edited_at

On [capacity-based](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/13872357395228#UUID-d0c73768-f31c-0510-0c3b-be5b46a76161 "Capacity-Based Pricing") plans, Phrase calculates consumption of Generative AI Capabilities in AI Units (AIUs). The table below lists the Generative AI Capabilities available via the Phrase platform and how their AIU consumption is calculated.

| Type | AIU-to-usage ratio | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Auto LQA](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14313477218588#UUID-be576442-c089-055d-5cba-4c0b50dea415) | 1 word = 1 AIU | This is calculated based on the total words in the target language; or, if the target language is a character based language (e.g., Japanese, Korean, Simplified or Traditional Chinese, Thai), the total number of characters uploaded divided by two. |
| [Auto Adapt](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/18807673145372#UUID-13f11225-b03e-7da3-bc9f-1db0de86ea75) | 1 word = 1 AIU | This is calculated based on the total words in the target language; or, if the target language is a character based language (e.g., Japanese, Korean, Simplified or Traditional Chinese, Thai), the total number of characters uploaded divided by two. |
| [MT Optimize](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a) | 1 word = 0.2 AIUs | This is calculated based on the total words in the target language; or, if the target language is a character based language (e.g., Japanese, Korean, Simplified or Traditional Chinese, Thai), the total number of characters uploaded divided by two.  To ensure timely processing, the recommended input limit is 5,000 words. |
| [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9) | 1 word = 0.25 AIUs | This is calculated based on the total words in the target language; or, if the target language is a character based language (e.g., Japanese, Korean, Simplified or Traditional Chinese, Thai), the total number of characters uploaded divided by two. |
| [AI Checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks") | 10 words = 1 AIU  Each additional AI check: 25% of the first check's cost | This is calculated from the number of unlocked (evaluated) source words, or, if the target language is character-based (e.g., Japanese, Korean, Simplified or Traditional Chinese, Thai), the total number of characters uploaded divided by two.  **Example**  The Quality profile is run on a job with 10,000 evaluated (unlocked) source words. Calculation:   ``` First check: 10,000 ÷ 10 = 1,000 AIUs Second check: 25% × 1,000 = 250 AIUs ```   Total consumption: 1,250 AIUs. |

AI unit consumption over time can be monitored by clicking on the Analytics button ![Access Analytics](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682536877468)on the Subscription overview tab in [Organization settings](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/6091807042076#UUID-de688ee0-29bf-a886-cf73-129e272a8613 "Phrase Platform Organizations").

---

### Platform API Access Token Management

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/22675071795996-Platform-API-Access-Token-Management  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-06-26T06:26:27Z  
> Labels: Administration, 2BTr

The Phrase Platform provides a unified API token management interface for generating and managing access tokens across all Phrase products.

Access tokens are used to access the [Phrase APIs](https://developers.phrase.com/en/home) without providing a username and password taking the place of login data. Specific actions can be performed within the API based on granted privileges.

Tokens are organization-specific. Platform users can view and manage tokens for the current [Phrase organization](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/6091807042076#UUID-de688ee0-29bf-a886-cf73-129e272a8613 "Phrase Platform Organizations") in the Access tokens tab of the [user profile settings](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709742592668#UUID-850152bf-7b70-0e42-f3bd-589525cad1fc "User Profile Settings").

Token expiration and scopes are fixed and cannot be edited. Tokens are limited to all available actions in the selected Phrase applications by default.

##### Generate API tokens

Each user can generate up to 20 access tokens. The API token/secret is valid for 730 days (2 years).

Platform API tokens are long-lived but cannot be used directly with product APIs or the [Strings CLI](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5808300599068#UUID-9439325a-ecf6-2f99-2c1e-1bf8197c757d). They must be exchanged for a short-lived [JSON Web Token](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JSON_Web_Token) (JWT) via the [OAuth token endpoint](https://developers.phrase.com/en/api/platform/authentication). The JWT access token expires after 4 hours.

To generate an access token, follow these steps from inside the Platform Dashboard:

1. From the user menu (top right), select Settings and click on Profile.

   The Profile settings page opens.
2. Select the Access tokens tab and click Generate token.

   The Generate access token window is displayed.
3. Provide a Token name and select one or more applications from the Service dropdown.
4. Click Generate token.

   The generated token is displayed in the One-time access token display! window.
5. Click Copy token to save it to the clipboard.

   ### Important

   For security reasons, the token will no longer be shown once the window is closed.

Created tokens are listed in the Access tokens tab with relevant information. Use the search bar or the Filter options to filter tokens by name or service.

To delete one or multiple tokens, select them and click Delete. Deleting a token revokes it permanently.

---

### Service Accounts

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/29488404284316-Service-Accounts  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-08-17T06:23:05Z

A service account supports integrations that do not depend on a personal user account. Each service account provisions a bot user scoped to the selected Phrase capabilities (products) and authenticates using the standard OAuth 2.0 client credentials flow, the standard machine-to-machine (M2M) authentication pattern.

##### Create a Service Account

To create a service account, follow these steps:

1. From the Dashboard, go to Organizations settings.

   The organization Details page opens.
2. Click the Service account tab.

   The Service accounts page opens.
3. Click Create service account.

   The Create service account page opens.
4. Provide the name and optionally, the Description.
5. Select at least one Phrase capability this service account will have access to and, optionally, select scopes for each capability from the dropdown. A service account can have access to one or multiple Phrase capabilities.
6. Click Create.

   The **Client ID** and **Client Secret** are displayed.

   ### Important

   Store the Client Secret securely. It cannot be retrieved again once the window closes.
7. Click Done to complete the process.

##### Generate an Access Token

The integration exchanges the Client ID and Client Secret for a short-lived access token:

```
POST /oauth2/token
grant_type=client_credentials
client_id=<client-id>
client_secret=<client-secret>
```

##### Bot Users

Bot users appear in the product user interface with a bot badge and cannot log in.

Bot users cannot:

- Create or modify human users.
- Create projects.
- Be assigned as project owners.
- Create jobs.

Bot users do not count toward seat quotas.

The only way to remove a bot user is to delete its Service Account. To delete a service account, hover over its row to reveal the ![Open More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511195164) menu and click Delete.

---

### Style Guides

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25124298060060-Style-Guides  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:36Z  
> Labels: 2BTr, edited_at

##### Important

Legacy style guides will be deprecated in Phrase TMS on **October 12, 2026.** From that date, new style guides in Phrase TMS are linked to a [content group](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups"), and Phrase extracts rules from them. Existing TMS projects keep their style guides and continue to work.

Phrase Strings and Phrase Studio continue to use style guides as before.

See the FAQ at the end of this article.

Style guides are centralized language guidelines that define how content should be written for a specific language or locale. They help ensure that tone, terminology, formatting, and output remain consistent across projects and teams.

Style guides are uploaded as Markdown (.md) files and stored in a shared library accessible from the Phrase Platform dashboard by selecting Assets in the left navigation menu. They can be attached and reused across projects in:

- [Phrase TMS](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-2b59cf4d-f4b7-2c84-a50a-d09e83d78194_bridgehead-id235438613416104 "Phrase TMS")
- [Phrase Strings](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-2b59cf4d-f4b7-2c84-a50a-d09e83d78194_bridgehead-id235438626503829 "Phrase Strings")
- [Phrase Studio](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-2b59cf4d-f4b7-2c84-a50a-d09e83d78194_bridgehead-id235438633741155 "Phrase Studio")

When a Markdown file is uploaded or a previous version is restored, Phrase automatically generates an AI-optimized version of the style guide. This version is used in AI workflows to improve [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9) and [MT Optimize](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a) results without requiring custom instructions for every job. The AI-friendly style guide enables Phrase AI services to adapt tone and formality to user preferences, apply required terminology and wording, and follow locale-specific conventions.

While the AI Translation Agent considers both term bases and style guides, style guides do not directly enforce terminology from a term base. The use of style guides does not incur any additional AI Unit (AIU) or credit cost.

Each style guide applies to exactly one locale (for example, `en-US` or `de-DE`). If multiple variants or use cases require different rules, separate style guides must be created.

Organizations can add up to 500 style guides.

##### Note

Style guides created before [content groups](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups") and [rules](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") were enabled for an organization are not linked to a content group. To link such a style guide, edit it and select a content group. Phrase then extracts rules from it. No file re-upload is required.

#### Permissions

- Only Platform organization [Administrators](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240 "Phrase User Management") can create, edit, delete, or set default style guides.
- Users with the Platform Member role (e.g. Linguists) can view the style guides from the Assets tab. They cannot make any modifications.
- Project Managers can attach style guides to projects.
- Linguists and Translators can view attached style guides in TMS and Strings editors.
- Users can download the latest version of a style guide as a markdown file from the Assets tab.

#### File and Structure Requirements

- One style guide in Markdown (.md) format per locale:

  - Maximum size: 150 KB
  - Images are not supported

    - Markdown (.md) files must be saved with UTF-8 encoding. Files saved with UTF-16 encoding, such as those saved from Notepad using the "Unicode" option, or files created by copying content directly from Microsoft Word, may use an incompatible encoding. Uploading a file with an incompatible encoding causes the style guide content to display with incorrect spacing or garbled characters. Before uploading, open the file in a text editor such as Notepad or VS Code, select Save As, and choose UTF-8 as the encoding.
- Recommended structure:

  1. Purpose and Scope
  2. Voice and Tone
  3. Grammar and Writing Rules
  4. Terminology (approved and forbidden terms)
  5. Locale Conventions
  6. Formatting Standards
  7. Content-Type Guidance
  8. Troubleshooting and Error Style (if applicable)

  Clear headings and structured rules improve both human readability and AI interpretation.

#### Example Style Guide File

Below is an example of a complete, non‑conflicting style guide that can be adapted as required:

Locale: en-US

Use case: SaaS B2B Product – Help Center and UI

```
# Style Guide: EN-US – Help Center Articles

## 1. Purpose & Scope

This style guide defines the writing standards for all **Help Center documentation** in **English (United States)**.

It applies to:

- How-to articles  
- Feature explanations  
- Troubleshooting guides  
- FAQ entries  

**Audience:** professional SaaS users in technical and business roles  
**Goal:** help users complete tasks quickly, confidently, and without confusion.

---

## 2. Voice & Tone

Help Center content should sound:

- **Clear and professional**
- **Supportive and solution-focused**
- **Confident, not promotional**

We write as a guide helping users succeed — not as marketing copy.

### Tone principles

| Do | Don’t |
|---|------|
| Be calm and direct | Be overly casual or chatty |
| Focus on next steps | Focus only on what went wrong |
| Use neutral language | Use sarcasm or humor |

**Examples**

- ✅ “If the connection fails, check your API token and try again.”  
- ❌ “Your token is wrong. Fix it.”

---

## 3. Grammar & Writing Style

### 3.1 Address the reader directly

Use **you** to make instructions clear.

- ✅ “You can manage users from the Admin page.”  
- ❌ “Users can be managed from the Admin page.”

---

### 3.2 Prefer active voice

Active voice is shorter and easier to follow.

- ✅ “Select **Save changes**.”  
- ❌ “Save changes should be selected.”

---

### 3.3 Keep sentences concise

- Aim for **25 words or fewer**
- One main idea per sentence
- Break long explanations into steps or bullets

---

### 3.4 Use plain language

Avoid unnecessary complexity.

- ✅ “Start a new project.”  
- ❌ “Initiate the creation of a new project.”

---

## 4. Terminology & Consistency

### 4.1 Use approved terms

Use product and feature names exactly as defined.

- Keep capitalization consistent  
- Do not invent synonyms for key concepts  

**Example**

- ✅ “workspace”  
- ❌ “space,” “project area,” “environment”

---

### 4.2 Define uncommon acronyms

Common terms (API, URL) do not need definition.  
Internal or uncommon acronyms should be explained on first use.

- ✅ “Single Sign-On (SSO)”  
- ❌ “SSO” without context

---

### 4.3 US English conventions

Always use **en-US spelling**.

- ✅ “customize,” “behavior”  
- ❌ “customise,” “behaviour”

---

## 5. Formatting & Markdown Standards

### 5.1 Headings

Use clear, task-based headings.

- ✅ “Reset your password”  
- ❌ “Password resetting process overview”

Heading hierarchy:

- `#` Article title  
- `##` Main sections  
- `###` Subsections only when needed  

---

### 5.2 Lists

Use numbered lists for sequences:

1. Open **Settings**  
2. Select **Billing**  
3. Choose **Change plan**  

Use bullets for options:

- Admins can manage users  
- Editors can update content  

---

### 5.3 UI elements

Format UI labels consistently:

- Buttons: **bold**
- Navigation paths: use arrows

Example:

Go to **Settings → Billing → Change plan**.

---

### 5.4 Links

Links must describe the destination.

- ✅ “See the billing guide”  
- ❌ “Click here”

---

## 6. Standard Article Structure

Every Help Center article should follow this structure:

### 1. Summary

Start with 1–2 sentences explaining the outcome.

> This article explains how to change your subscription plan.

---

### 2. Prerequisites (optional)

List requirements upfront.

- Admin permissions  
- Active subscription  

---

### 3. Step-by-step instructions

Steps should be:

- Action-oriented  
- One action per step  
- Written as commands  

Example:

1. Go to **Settings**.  
2. Select **Billing**.  
3. Choose **Change plan**.  

---

### 4. Expected result

Tell the user what should happen.

> Your new plan takes effect immediately after confirmation.

---

### 5. Next steps (optional)

Provide related actions or links.

- Manage invoices  
- Update payment method  

---

## 7. Troubleshooting & Errors

### 7.1 Be reassuring

- ✅ “We couldn’t connect. Please try again.”  
- ❌ “Connection failed. Critical error.”

---

### 7.2 Focus on solutions

Always include what the user should do next.

- Check credentials  
- Confirm permissions  
- Contact support if needed  

---

### 7.3 Never blame the user

Avoid language like:

- “You did something wrong”  
- “Invalid input” (without explanation)

Preferred:

> “The token may be expired. Generate a new one and retry.”

---

## 8. Do / Don’t Summary

### Do

- Write task-focused, step-based content  
- Use consistent terminology  
- Keep sentences short and direct  
- Use descriptive headings and links  
- Maintain a calm, supportive tone  

### Don’t

- Add marketing language  
- Use idioms or slang  
- Mix terms for the same concept  
- Blame the user in troubleshooting  
- Write long paragraphs without structure  

---

## Example (Preferred)

To change your plan:

1. Go to **Settings → Billing**  
2. Select **Change plan**  
3. Choose an option and select **Confirm**
```

#### Manage Style Guides

Style guides are created and managed in the shared library accessible from the Phrase Platform dashboard by selecting Assets in the left navigation menu. The Style guides page lists all style guides available in your organization.

If logged in to Phrase TMS, Phrase Strings, or Phrase Studio, select Style guides from the left navigation to open the shared library.

##### Create a Style Guide

To create a new style guide as Administrator, follow these steps:

1. In the Style guides page, select New style guide.

   The Create a style guide page is displayed.
2. Configure Language, Name and Description (optional).

   Name must be unique.
3. Select a content group from the dropdown.

   Phrase extracts rules from the style guide. In Phrase TMS, the rules are applied through the content group.
4. Drag and drop or select Upload file to upload the style guide as a Markdown (.md) file.
5. Click Create style guide.

   Phrase automatically generates the AI‑friendly version from the uploaded file. This might take a few seconds.

   The new style guide is added to the Style guides page.
6. Optionally, select Set as default.

   When attaching style guides to new projects, Phrase suggests the default style guide if no specific guide is set. The suggestion can be overridden.

   ### Tip

   Remove any language-specific rules from the default style guide.

##### Edit or Delete Style Guides

Style guides can be updated, versioned, shared, or removed. From the Style guides page, use the More actions ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511263644) menu next to a listed style guide to:

- Set as default

  Mark the style guide as the suggested default when no specific guide is selected in new projects. The default suggestion can be overridden at project level.
- Delete

  Deleting a style guide does not retroactively change completed or ongoing jobs. Only the latest version of a style guide can be attached to new projects.

  ### Tip

  Before deleting a style guide, verify:

  - Whether it is still attached to active projects or templates
  - Whether it must be retained for compliance or historical reference
- Copy public link

  Generate a read-only link for sharing with external stakeholders who do not have a Phrase account.

In the Style guides page, select the pencil icon ![Edit](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682527370524) next to a style guide to open the Edit style guide page and update its metadata, Markdown file or default status.

To link a style guide to a content group, open the Edit style guide page and select a content group. Phrase extracts rules from the style guide.

An optional description of the changes can be added before saving to include it in the version history. A new version is created only when the Markdown file is replaced. This version applies only to new jobs or to jobs that have not yet started in projects where the style guide is used.

##### Manage Version History

Style guides maintain a version history in case of changes to existing versions.

To view the version history and restore versions of a style guide, follow these steps:

1. In the Style guides page, click on a style guide in the list to open its detail page.
2. Select Version history from the More actions ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511263644) at the top of the detail page.

   The Version history panel is displayed.
3. Select an older version listed in the Previous versions section.

   The older version is displayed.
4. If required, select Edit from this version in the Version history.

   The previous version is restored to create a new active version based on it.

Restoring or editing from an earlier version does not overwrite history.

#### Use Style Guides in Projects

Once a style guide has been created in the library, it can be attached to projects in Phrase TMS, Phrase Strings, and Phrase Studio.

General behavior across products:

- Style guides are configured per target language and apply to the entire project. They are applied during pre-translation when using the AI Translation Agent, or as a post-editing step when using MT Optimize.
- When a new version of the style guide is created, it applies only to new jobs. Ongoing jobs continue using the version active at the time of creation.
- AI features use the attached style guide automatically when supported. Style guides do not affect locked segments, as MT Optimize does not modify them. By default, the AI Translation Agent also leaves segments from a Translation Memory (TM) unaffected, though this can be configured in the pre-translation settings.

###### Phrase TMS

Style guides can be attached to a [project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709748435484#UUID-30234837-9796-c6f2-1a21-a623b67ac6cc) or [project template](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709647439772#UUID-94346683-ae2a-16b5-7e16-94a554612687).

- From October 12, 2026, style guides are applied to TMS projects through [content groups](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups"). Assign the project or project template to a content group. The rules of that content group are applied automatically.
- In project settings, the style guide field is read-only. It shows the message: "The Style Guide is automatically assigned based on the Content Group and can't be edited."
- Existing projects, project templates, and cloned projects keep their current style guide and continue to work.
- Changes to project templates affect only newly created projects and [jobs](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709686763420#UUID-f360116d-841e-01a6-c030-b509327fa289). Existing projects are not retroactively updated.
- Attached style guides are visible to [linguists](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709735838492#UUID-911ee8d4-ecc1-0c38-8f71-7c22f1646545) in the Resources ![paperclip.jpeg](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682527396764) pane of the [CAT web editor](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709683890204#UUID-b7f5d899-ec2b-4352-a151-d45f6cfb5cd8) as a read-only resource.

  - Vendors in [shared projects](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709703948444#UUID-2fb330ab-e5ad-e017-5972-a9a75c1e0360) cannot modify a buyer’s assigned style guide.
  - In shared job scenarios, vendors can use the assigned style guide but cannot change project-level configuration.

###### Phrase Strings

- In a [project page](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5784094677404#UUID-67ae68da-d206-0281-f708-35cfdfb52252), navigate to the Style guides tab and select a style guide per target [locale](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5818281650204#UUID-ecfff114-7e61-c99d-f630-6f7ceca104f8).
- Attached style guides are visible to translators at the key level in the Style guides menu of the [Strings editor sidebar](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/11155533567388#UUID-3e5ac7d6-2a26-80e7-3555-dd2769217e29).

Nothing changes on October 12, 2026. Content groups are not used in Phrase Strings. Attach style guides as before. No action is needed.

###### Phrase Studio

- When [creating a project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/21177186715420#UUID-df5d31ff-16d9-6310-bc96-0818fca7a7d9), select a style guide for each target language added to the project.

  The system may automatically pre-select the most relevant style guide based on locale matching. The pre-selection can always be overridden or cleared.
- The AI-friendly version of the style guide is applied behind the scenes as contextual input for AI Translation Agent workflows.

Nothing changes on October 12, 2026. Content groups are not used in Phrase Studio. Attach style guides as before. No action is needed.

###### Style Guide API

Style guides are also accessible through a dedicated [Style Guide public API](https://developers.phrase.com/en/api/style-guides/introduction), separate from the Phrase TMS API. This API supports creating, updating, retrieving, searching, and versioning style guides programmatically. New integrations should use the v2 endpoints, POST /api/v2/styleguides and PUT /api/v2/styleguides/{id}, which link a style guide to a Content Group. The API is region specific:

- EU: https://eu.phrase.com/styleguide
- US: https://us.phrase.com/styleguide

The v1 endpoints remain available.

Authentication requires exchanging a Phrase Platform API token for a JWT, as described in the Platform Authentication guide in the developer documentation.

#### FAQ: Style Guides after October 12, 2026

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| **1.** | What changes on October 12, 2026? |
|  | In Phrase TMS, new style guides are converted into rules in a content group. The style guide field in project settings becomes read-only, and style guides are applied through content groups. |
| **2.** | Which products are affected? |
|  | Only Phrase TMS. In Phrase Strings and Phrase Studio, nothing changes, and no action is needed. |
| **3.** | Will existing TMS projects stop working? |
|  | No. Existing TMS projects keep their style guides and continue to work as before. |
| **4.** | Is re-uploading style guides required? |
|  | No. Edit the style guide and select a content group. Phrase extracts the rules. Then assign the TMS project to the same content group. |
| **5.** | Can translation output in TMS change? |
|  | Yes, because the rules are a new set of instructions. Review the rules and test them on one project first. |

---

### Quality Profiles

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25865045974300-Quality-Profiles  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:37Z  
> Labels: Quality Assurance, 2BTr, AI, edited_at

#### Available for

- Team, Professional, Business, Enterprise and Software UI/UX plans

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) for licensing questions.

##### Important

Quality profiles are being replaced by [Content Groups](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups") and their current version will be deprecated on **October 12, 2026.**

Existing profiles continue to work during the transition.

Quality profiles allow organizations to define consistent, AI-driven quality checks for multilingual content.

A Quality profile defines up to three AI checks that include specific review criteria for the AI to evaluate segment quality. Review criteria instruct the AI on what to look for in the translated segments to determine whether they meet the quality standards.

Quality evaluation is enforced in Phrase TMS during [pre-translation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709717749788#UUID-a982e5f9-9aa2-434f-116c-17b822d35343) and can be applied once per [job](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709686763420#UUID-f360116d-841e-01a6-c030-b509327fa289) at [project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709748435484#UUID-30234837-9796-c6f2-1a21-a623b67ac6cc) or [project template](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709647439772#UUID-94346683-ae2a-16b5-7e16-94a554612687) level. During pre-translation, segments are evaluated according to the selected profile. If a segment passes all AI checks, it is automatically locked. If it does not, it remains editable and the detected issues can be reviewed in the [CAT web editor](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709683890204#UUID-b7f5d899-ec2b-4352-a151-d45f6cfb5cd8).

[Eingebettetes Video/Inhalt](https://www.youtube.com/embed/j45dmdetFyA)

#### Availability and AI Usage

Quality profiles are not enabled by default. To use the feature, the organization must:

- Have an active Phrase Platform subscription that includes [AI Units](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14032731809052#UUID-244d7872-8d03-3335-c502-dea9b1b12369 "AI Unit Calculation") (AIUs).
- Request access to enable the feature. Select Quality profiles in the left navigation menu of the Phrase Platform to unlock the feature.

  Only Platform [Administrators and Owners](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240 "Phrase User Management") can request access.

Each AI check evaluation consumes AIUs on capacity-based plans, and credits on credit-based plans. Only unlocked (evaluated) words are counted.

#### Limits

- Up to three AI checks can be added to any Quality profile.
- Review criteria can contain maximum 2000 characters.
- Quality profiles are executed once per job during pre-translation. If the job was not automatically pre-translated on job import, quality evaluation can be triggered later via manual pre-translation from the project list page. If manually triggered, quality evaluation is limited to the first workflow step.
- Profiles must be created in the Quality profiles page before they can be selected in pre-translation settings.

#### Permissions

- Only Platform Owners and Administrators can configure Quality profiles and access the Insights tab in the Quality profiles page.
- TMS [Project Managers](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709756503196#UUID-f5990e2a-6aa9-ce27-fe52-bdad461726e6) can apply Quality profiles to pre‑translation.
- TMS [Linguists](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709735838492#UUID-911ee8d4-ecc1-0c38-8f71-7c22f1646545) can only view quality evaluation results in the CAT web editor.

#### Configure Quality Profiles

After access is enabled, Quality profiles and AI checks can be created and managed in the Quality profiles page of the Phrase Platform that is accessible by selecting Quality profiles in the left navigation menu.

##### Create Quality Profiles

To create a Quality profile, follow these steps:

1. Click New Quality profile.

   The New Quality profile page is displayed.
2. Click Edit to change the profile name and Save it.

   ### Tip

   The profile name does not need to be unique. It is recommended to choose a name that reflects the content type and/or target language it applies to. Note that there is no automatic matching between language-specific Quality profiles and language pairs in a project.
3. Click Add AI Check to configure the AI checks for the new profile:

   - Select Create new to [create a new AI check](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0_bridgehead-id235449207572097 "Create and Test AI Checks") in the Create AI Check section.
   - Select Add existing to find and add existing AI checks from the Add AI checks side panel.
4. Repeat step 3 until all desired checks have been added, then click Create profile at the top right of the page.

   The new profile appears in the Quality profiles list.

##### Create and Test AI Checks

To create a new AI check, follow these steps:

1. When [adding an AI check](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0_bridgehead-id235440683168456 "Create Quality Profiles") in new or existing profiles, choose Create new.

   The Create AI Check section is displayed.
2. Provide a Name.

   The AI check name does not have to be unique, but it should be descriptive enough to differentiate it.
3. Enter [Review criteria](https://support.phrase.com#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0_bridgehead-id235449271838515 "Best Practices for Review Criteria").

   Optionally, click View examples to see and use predefined templates.
4. Click Test AI Check to expand the relevant section and validate the AI check before saving.
5. Select the desired Source language and Target language, then enter source and target text.

   ### Tip

   The Target text should intentionally contain errors that violate the review criteria.
6. Click Run test.

   The AI evaluates the target text according to the review criteria and displays detected issues in the Issues found section.

   If the AI does not find the expected issues, refine the review criteria and retest.
7. To test additional languages with the same criteria, click Add language and repeat steps 5 and 6.
8. Click Save check to add it to the Quality profile.

   The check becomes available for reuse in other profiles.

##### Best Practices for Review Criteria

Review criteria determine the effectiveness of an AI check.

Multiple criteria can be included in a single AI check and can be grouped by theme (e.g., tone and terminology) or mixed within a single check. Both approaches are valid as long as instructions are not contradictory. Instructions can include rules applicable only to specific target languages.

Best practices include:

1. Specify content type and tone (for example, technical, marketing, legal; formal or persuasive).
2. List aspects to evaluate, such as terminology usage, style, clarity, structure, or brand voice.
3. Avoid ambiguity and vague terms.
4. Limit criteria to the most important requirements. Avoid overloading the AI with too many requirements. If needed, create separate checks for different criteria.
5. Use templates provided in the UI as starting points.
6. Refine criteria progressively. Start with general guidance, then increase specificity or add examples if results are not accurate enough.

   - Example (general): `Follow locale standards for Czech currencies.`
   - Example (specific): `Define currency format, separators, or symbol placement.`
   - Example (with output): `Use '1 000,50 Kč' instead of 'CZK 1,000.50'.`
7. Write criteria that can be evaluated at the segment level. Avoid rules that depend on context outside a single segment (e.g., first mention in a document or document structure such as titles or subtitles).

##### Manage Quality Profiles and AI Checks

Quality profiles and AI checks can be edited, removed or deleted. From the Quality profiles page, click on a listed profile to open the Edit Quality profile page:

- Click Edit to change the name of the profile.
- Use the AI Checks section to change the assigned AI checks by removing existing checks or adding new ones.
- For AI checks listed inside the profile:

  - Click Edit from the three dots ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511357852) menu next to a check to update its name or review criteria.
  - Click Remove from profile from the three dots ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511357852) menu to remove the check from the profile. The check remains available for use in other profiles.
  - Click Permanently delete from the three dots ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682511357852) menu to delete the check from all profiles.

Select Quality profile(s) in the Quality profiles page and click Delete to delete them. Deleting a profile does not delete its underlying AI checks.

#### Use and Monitor Quality Profiles

Once Quality profiles are configured, they can be applied during pre-translation and reviewed in the CAT web editor.

To apply a Quality profile during pre-translation, follow these steps:

1. When [creating or editing a project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/10825044285340#UUID-3ec95012-fe40-e725-1b2e-67b8d303676d) or project template, navigate to the Pre‑translation settings.
2. Under Lock segment for, select Meets Quality profile review criteria.

   - Quality profile evaluation follows the locking sequence defined in pre-translation settings. It is applied only to segments that were not locked by previous steps (translation memory matches, non‑translatables, QPS score, etc.).
3. Choose a Quality profile from the drop-down list.
4. Save the settings.

   The Quality profile evaluates segments during pre-translation. If a segment passes all checks, it is automatically locked.

Quality profile evaluation only runs against translated segments. If a job has no translations, no evaluation results are generated.

If jobs were imported without automatic pre-translation, quality evaluation can be triggered manually from the project's job list. To run a manual quality evaluation, follow these steps:

1. Select the job(s) to evaluate.

   Quality evaluation can be run on a job in any workflow step.
2. Click the Pre-translate button.
3. From the dropdown menu, select Pre-translate where empty.

   The Pre-translate window is displayed.
4. Under Lock segment for, select Meets Quality profile review criteria and the desired Quality profile to use for the evaluation.
5. Click Pre-translate to begin the process.

   All segments in the selected jobs that have not been previously locked are evaluated, based on the criteria in the chosen Quality profile.

**Sharing-specific behavior**

- When a [project is shared](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709703948444-Sharing-TMS#sharing-projects-0-4) with a Vendor, the Vendor can view quality evaluation results in the AI checks tab if quality evaluation was previously run in the Buyer organization. However, a Vendor cannot view the AI checks defined in the Buyer's quality profile, as these are only visible from the Buyer account.
- A Vendor can run quality evaluation using their own quality profiles in shared projects but cannot use the Buyer's quality profiles.

##### Review Results in the CAT Web Editor

After running pre-translation, open the job in the CAT web editor to review results. Results are only accessible in the workflow step where quality evaluation was run.

Locked segments show a lock icon with a tooltip explaining that the segment was locked after passing quality evaluation review criteria.

For segments that did not pass quality evaluation and were not locked, use the [Quality Assurance (QA) pane](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709694857372#UUID-bccfa2a1-8f4d-d877-fc52-4d14891aba36) to understand why a segment failed and decide whether to revise the translation manually:

1. In the CAT web editor, select the QA pane.
2. Click the AI checks tab.

   Quality profile warnings in the AI checks tab are separate from QA warnings. QA warnings appear in the QA checks tab when QA is run. Quality profile warnings only appear once segments have been pre-translated and evaluated.

   Each failed AI check displays a description of the issue for review.

   ### Note

   Issues remain attached to a segment even if the segment is updated. They are removed only when a segment is [split or joined](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709678012828#UUID-753af50b-27d0-e386-418a-452ed30e0083).

AI check results are saved on the specific workflow step where quality evaluation ran and do not carry over into later steps. For example, in a two-step workflow where [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9) runs in the first step and linguists work in a second step, AI checks generated during the first step do not appear in the second step.

Quality evaluation can also be re-run from the CAT Web Editor via Run AI checks in the Quality Assurance pane to check segments after edits.

- When quality evaluation is triggered from the CAT Web Editor, the Quality profile configured in pre-translate settings is applied.
- Previously locked segments are excluded from the quality evaluation run; prior quality evaluation results are replaced with the new ones.
- Running quality evaluation from the CAT Web Editor does not lock segments that pass, allowing Linguists to continue working on them.

##### Quality Profile Insights

The Insights tab on the Quality profiles page, accessible from the Platform dashboard, provides an overview of how Quality profiles affect the organization workflow(s).

Metrics include:

- Locked words percentage

  The share of words automatically locked by Quality profiles out of the total words processed.
- Total volume

  The number of segments or words evaluated by Quality profiles.
- The percentage of words automatically approved and locked by Quality profiles.
- AI Units consumed

  The total AIUs used by Quality profiles.

Use filters to analyze specific periods or Quality profiles. This information helps assess the effectiveness of the Quality profile configuration and manage AIU consumption.

---

### Content Groups

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812-Content-Groups  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:38Z  
> Labels: edited_at

#### Available for

- Phrase TMS (API support)
- UI access available upon request

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) or your Customer Success Manager for licensing questions and UI access requests.

##### Note

Content Groups, [Rules](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules"), and [AI Checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks") are available in the Phrase user interface upon request: any Platform Administrator or Owner can manage them directly in the product once UI access is enabled for the organization. The full flow also remains available via API for teams that integrate programmatically.

A content group organizes projects by content type, such as marketing copy, UI strings, or legal text. It defines rules and AI quality checks once at the group level, and every project added to the group applies them automatically. This removes repeated per-project setup and the risk of projects drifting out of sync.

##### What Is a Content Group

A content group is a platform-level, organization-scoped container that projects and language assets are linked to. It represents one type of content that shares the same tone, terminology, and quality bar.

Content that differs in type, such as marketing pages and legal disclaimers, usually needs its own tone, terminology, and quality bar. Previously, this required configuring style guides and quality checks separately for every project. Content groups replace that per-project setup: configure a content type once, and every project of that type inherits it automatically.

Content groups are language-agnostic: a group is not tied to any specific language. Language-specific rules are handled at the rule level, so the same group can apply different rules per language where needed.

##### How Everything Is Matched: Content Group and Language

Content groups are the single organizing principle across the platform. Everything resolves through the (content group, language) pair.

- The content side:

  Text lives in jobs. Every job carries a language (a source-target language pair, for example, `fr-FR` to `en-US`). Jobs live in projects, and each project is assigned to exactly one content group, for example, *Legal*. Because a job inherits its project's content group and carries its own language, every job has a (content group, language) attribution.
- The platform side:

  The language assets, rules, and the AI checks derived from them are attributed to content groups and languages.

When an AI capability runs on a job (inside a product or via API), Phrase uses the (content group, language) match to gather every asset attributed to that same pair and apply it automatically. There is no separate quality profile to select and no need to attach guidelines job by job.

Example: A user launches the [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9) for a French job in a project that belongs to the *Legal* content group. Phrase gathers all rules attributed to *French* and *Legal* and applies them during translation, then uses the AI checks derived from those rules during quality evaluation.

A content group holds rules for any number of languages at once, and each rule keeps its own language attribution. An organization with three content types and ten languages needs three content groups, one per content type. Each content group holds the rules for all ten languages, and the (content group, language) match still applies the correct rules to each job.

##### How a Content Group Relates to Style Guides, Rules, and AI Checks

A content group ties language guidance to the projects it governs:

1. Upload a [style guide](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") or create rules manually. Phrase extracts a set of **rules** and links them to the content group, and to a specific language where a rule is language-specific.
2. From each eligible rule, Phrase creates one **AI check** automatically, a 1:1 mapping.
3. A **quality profile** is the full set of AI checks for that content group. It matches the group one-to-one and is assigned automatically. It is not built or selected manually.

In short, the content group a project belongs to determines which rules and AI checks apply to it.

A content group accepts style guides for more than one language. Phrase links the rules extracted from each style guide to the language of that style guide, so one content group accumulates rules for multiple languages over time. For example, uploading a German style guide and a French style guide into the Marketing content group creates two sets of rules: one linked to German and one linked to French. Both sets belong to the same content group.

Uploading a new style guide for a content group and language combination that already has one replaces the existing rules extracted from it. Phrase shows a warning before the replacement.

##### Which Features Use Content Groups

Content groups are used by:

- [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9)
- [MT Optimize](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a)
- [AI Checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks")

##### Permissions

- Only users with the [Platform Admin](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240_UUID-b1e37850-219b-c7cf-8cd0-087b0b39bc44 "Platform User Management") or the Owner role can create, edit, and delete content groups and manage the rules and checks inside them.
- Platform members (for example, TMS Linguists) can view the rules and checks that apply to their content but cannot change them.

#### Managing Content Groups in the UI

##### Open the Content Groups Overview

From the Dashboard, click Content groups to display the content groups overview.

Phrase ships with a few predefined content groups (for example, *Marketing*, *UI*, and *Legal*) to avoid starting from an empty state. Use these as they are, rename them, or create new ones.

##### Create a Content Group

To create a new content group:

1. On the Content groups page, click New content group.

   The New content group window opens.
2. Enter a Name.
3. Click Create.

   The new group appears as a card on the overview.

##### Rename or Delete a Content Group

On the group's page, open the *More Actions* menu ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682537156252) next to its name:

- Select Edit to rename the content group. Renaming a group updates its name everywhere, including on every rule attributed to it.
- Select Delete to delete the content group.

  ### Caution

  **Deleting a content group affects its rules.**

  - A rule linked only to that group is deleted along with it, and its AI check is deleted too.
  - A rule linked to several groups is kept and only loses its link to the deleted group.

  Review a group's rules before deleting the group. Phrase shows a notification before and after the deletion.

##### Open a Content Group's Detail View

Select a group card to open its detail view. Everything that belongs to the group is managed through two tabs:

- Rules

  Create, edit, enable, disable, reassign, and delete the [rules](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") that apply to this group.
- Quality profiles

  View the [AI checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks") that run during quality evaluation for this group.

##### Linking a Style Guide to a Content Group

To link a style guide to a content group, navigate to Assets in the Dashboard, open the Style guides tab and select the content group when uploading the style guide.

Uploading extracts rules automatically and links them to the selected content group. If a style guide already exists for this content group and language pair, uploading again replaces it and re-extracts rules.

#### Assigning a Project to a Content Group

A project's content group is set directly in the project or project template in Phrase TMS.

To assign a project to a content group:

1. Create or edit a [project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709748435484#UUID-30234837-9796-c6f2-1a21-a623b67ac6cc) (or a [project template](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709647439772#UUID-94346683-ae2a-16b5-7e16-94a554612687)).
2. In the Project Details section, select the content group from the Content group dropdown.

   The dropdown lists all the organization's content groups.
3. Click through the project settings pages and configure them as needed.
4. Click Create project or Save project if editing an existing project.

A project can belong to only **one** content group at a time, so there is never ambiguity about which rules apply.

A project's content group can be changed at any time, or removed to leave the project ungrouped.

##### Tip

If the Content group field is not visible, it may not be enabled for your organization yet. Contact your Customer Success Manager.

Once a project belongs to a content group, translation, post-editing, and quality checks are automated through the group. Style guides and quality profiles no longer need to be added or selected job by job.

##### Key Things to Know

- A project can belong to only one content group at a time.
- A content group can relate to any number of projects.
- Content groups have a flat structure. There are no subgroups. For subcategories, create standalone groups with descriptive names.
- Content groups are language-agnostic; the language always comes from the job.

##### Limits

- A maximum of **50** content groups can be created per organization.
