### Rules

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788-Rules  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:39Z  
> Labels: edited_at

#### Available for

- Phrase TMS (API support)
- UI access available upon request

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) or your Customer Success Manager for licensing questions and UI access requests.

##### Note

Rules are the new single source of truth for language guidelines, and [AI checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks") are generated from them.

[Style guides](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25124298060060#UUID-2b59cf4d-f4b7-2c84-a50a-d09e83d78194 "Style Guides") still exist as the starting point for creating rules, rather than what Phrase reads from directly. Rules and AI checks work alongside the previous style guides and quality profiles experiences during the transition.

[Quality profiles](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25865045974300#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0 "Quality Profiles") in their current form will be deprecated on **October 12, 2026.**

**Organizations without access to rules, before October 12, 2026:** Customers upload style guides and assign them in project settings. Customers manually create quality profiles, write AI checks inside each profile, and use them during the quality evaluation process.

**Organizations with access to rules, and every organization after October 12, 2026:** Phrase converts uploaded style guides into rules, which automatically match the content group that the style guide and project belong to. During the quality evaluation process, Phrase automatically assigns quality profiles, a set of AI checks based on the content group. Customers can select which AI checks to switch on and off. Style guides uploaded before gaining access to rules become read-only. Phrase does not delete or convert them. To convert a style guide created before rules were enabled for the organization into rules, re-upload it and choose a content group during the upload.

Rules are centralized language guidelines that define how content is written for a specific [content group](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups") and language. They keep tone, terminology, formatting, and wording consistent across projects and teams.

Rules are the single source of truth for language guidelines. Create rules directly, or upload a [style guide](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25124298060060#UUID-2b59cf4d-f4b7-2c84-a50a-d09e83d78194 "Style Guides") and extract rules from it. Either way, Phrase reads from the rules, not the style guide itself, when translating content and running quality checks.

#### How Rules Fit into the Platform

Phrase matches everything by content group and language. A project belongs to one content group, for example, *Marketing* or *Legal*, and each job carries a language, for example `en-US` or `de-DE`. When an AI capability runs on a job, Phrase gathers all rules for that content group and language and applies them automatically.

#### Permissions

- Only [Platform Administrators](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5793349215900#UUID-e2b3dca6-b062-9f47-48a0-c3502f7af240 "Phrase User Management") and **Owners** can create, edit, and delete rules and content groups.
- Platform **members** (for example, TMS Linguists) can view rules and their style guides from the Assets tab and in the editor. They cannot make changes.
- Any user can download the latest version of a style guide as a Markdown file from Assets.

#### Create Rules

There are two ways to create rules: they can be extracted from a style guide or created manually. Both options are available in the UI.

##### From a Style Guide

Uploading a style guide is the fastest way to create a full set of rules. The style guide is only the starting point: once rules are extracted, Phrase works from the rules.

1. From Dashboard, click Assets, then the Style guides tab.

   The Style guides page is displayed.
2. Select Upload style guide.

   The Upload a style guide page opens.
3. Choose the Language (locale) the guide applies to.
4. Choose the Content group the guide belongs to.
5. Enter a Name (up to 50 characters) and, optionally, a Description.
6. Drag and drop or select Upload file to upload the style guide file.

   Max file size is 150 KB. Supported file types: Markdown .MD, markdown, or a .TXT file.

   A preview of the file is shown.
7. Click Save and extract rules.

   Phrase extracts the rules. Once extracted, the Rules are extracted window opens.
8. Click Review rules to open the extracted set in the content group for review or select Skip for now to review them later from the content group's Rules tab.

Extracted rules take the style guide's content group and language. The style guide and its rules stay linked. Sharing a style guide externally always shares its rules as well.

##### Manually

Create rules directly when no style guide exists yet or when guidelines are maintained by hand.

1. From Dashboard, click Content groups.

   The Content groups page opens.
2. Select the group to which the rule should apply by clicking the group tile.

   The group's Rules tab is displayed.
3. Click New rule.

   The New rule window opens.
4. Enter the Rule text.

   A rule can hold up to 450 characters.

   Write one clear, self-contained instruction, for example, "Always address the reader with the formal 'Sie' in German."
5. Choose the Content group and Language the rule applies to. More than one of each can be selected.
6. Leave Also create a matching AI Check and link it to this rule checked for this rule to be verified during [quality evaluation](https://support.phrase.com/document/preview/136822#UUID-7cd37781-f22f-39d4-f160-11d5af14010b).
7. Click Create rule.

AI capabilities, such as [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9) and [MT Optimize](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a), automatically use rules to improve results: adapting tone and formality, applying required wording, and following language conventions. Using rules does not add any [AI Unit](https://support.phrase.com/document/preview/124103#UUID-9d1cb566-c6e4-d4fe-c5d8-0b547601bb09) (AIU) or credit cost.

For rules, and subsequently AI checks to be applied, the language codes must match exactly to language codes used in a project where the content group with the rules is assigned. For example, if a rule uses `IT-IT`, the project needs to use `IT-IT` as well.

#### Reuse Rules Across Content Groups and Languages

Rules extracted from a style guide belong to that style guide's language and content group. Uploading a new version replaces the linked rules with a freshly extracted set. Rules added, edited, or reassigned manually remain unaffected, and previous style guide versions stay accessible.

Manually created rules are independent from style guides and can be assigned to more than one content group and more than one language. To apply a rule everywhere, assign it to all content groups and all languages. This reuses a guideline instead of recreating it for every combination.

For example, universal tone-of-voice rules apply to every content group, plus a few legal-only rules apply to the *Legal Content Group*. When a legal job runs in French, both sets apply.

#### Work with Rules

After rules are created, manage them from the relevant content group's Rules tab. Each row shows the rule text, the content group(s) and language(s) it applies to, a style guide name when created from a style guide, last modified by, its status, and whether it has an AI check.

- **Enable or disable a rule**

  Turning off a rule removes it from AI workflows and disables its AI check, so quality evaluation skips it. Re-enabling a rule does not automatically re-enable its AI check.
- **Edit a rule**

  Select Edit from the three-dot ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682505186460) menu to change its text.

  Editing a rule that came from a style guide breaks its link to that style guide, and the rule becomes a manual rule. A confirmation window appears before the change is applied, keeping it clear which rules still come from a style guide. The rule's source changes to the name of the user who made the change.
- **Reassign a rule**

  Change the content groups and language a rule belongs to at any time. If the rule was created from a style guide, reassigning it also breaks its link and turns it into a manual rule, so this is best avoided for extracted rules unless necessary.
- **Delete a rule**

  Select Delete from the three-dot ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682505186460) menu to delete it.

  Deleting a rule also removes its AI check. It does not affect the source style guide or any other rules.

Rule changes take effect immediately for jobs already running in that content group. Make changes only when no jobs for that content group are active, and check rules before starting new jobs.

Deleting a content group deletes any rule linked only to that group. A rule linked to several groups keeps the rule and only loses the link to the deleted group.

#### How Rules Connect to AI Checks

Rules are also the foundation of automated quality checks. Phrase creates one AI check from each eligible rule, a one-to-one mapping. AI checks cannot be created from scratch; every check comes from a rule. Where an AI check cannot be created from a rule, Phrase skips the check and shows the reason.

Because checks come from rules, managing rules manages checks: disabling a rule disables its check, and deleting a rule deletes its check.

A [quality profile](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25865045974300#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0 "Quality Profiles") is the full set of AI checks for one content group. It matches a content group one-to-one, and Phrase assigns it automatically.

#### Limits

- A maximum of **50 rules** can apply to one content group and language combination. Aim for fewer, higher-impact rules rather than exhaustive lists.
- A manually created rule can be up to **450 characters**.
- Organizations can upload up to **500 style guides**, one per content group and language combination. Uploading a new style guide for a combination that already has one replaces it and re-extracts its rules. A warning is displayed.
- A style guide file can be up to **150 KB** (about 150,000 characters).

---

### AI Checks

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300-AI-Checks  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-08T06:23:33Z

#### Available for

- Phrase TMS (API support)
- UI access available upon request

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) or your Customer Success Manager for licensing questions and UI access requests.

##### Note

AI checks replace the previous [quality profiles](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25865045974300#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0 "Quality Profiles") experience. Quality profiles are no longer created or selected manually; Phrase assigns one automatically based on the project's [content group](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups").

[Quality profiles](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/25865045974300#UUID-77192897-7104-9fcb-e1c1-73b82f259cb0 "Quality Profiles") will be deprecated on **October 12, 2026.**

AI checks are automated checks that run during quality evaluation. Each AI check comes from a [rule](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") and returns a *true* or *false* verdict for the content it checks.

Phrase creates AI checks from rules automatically, not by hand. Managing [rules](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") manages AI checks.

Everything is matched by [content group](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818060694812#UUID-b62dfd39-77f2-07a8-572f-4a0a263f87e3 "Content Groups") and language. A project belongs to one content group, and each job has a language. When quality evaluation runs on a job, Phrase applies the AI checks for that job's content group and language automatically, with no separate step to add or select checks per project.

#### AI Check Origin

AI checks come from rules, never from scratch. Phrase creates one AI check from each eligible rule, a one-to-one mapping. Because every check comes from a rule, managing rules decides which checks run:

- Disabling a rule disables its AI check, so quality evaluation skips it.
- Re-enabling a rule does not automatically re-enable its AI check.
- Deleting a rule deletes its AI check.

Rules are the single source of truth and AI checks always follow them, so guidance and checks cannot drift apart.

When [pre-translation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709717749788#UUID-a982e5f9-9aa2-434f-116c-17b822d35343) uses a capability that does not apply rules, for example, [Phrase Next GenMT](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14299433827996#UUID-0818c20e-733b-2a4c-701e-699937ba1184) instead of [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9), AI checks still require rules first. Checks always come from rules; no standalone AI check exists.

#### Permissions

- Only Platform Administrators and Owners can manage the rules that produce AI checks.
- Platform members (for example, TMS Linguists) can view the checks that apply to their content. They cannot change them.

#### Viewing AI Checks in the UI

AI checks are generated and assigned automatically. The checks that apply to any content group, and the reasons behind them, are always visible.

1. From Dashboard, click Content groups.
2. Select the content group to view its AI checks.
3. Open the Quality profiles tab. AI checks that run during quality evaluation for this group, grouped by language, are displayed.

Each AI check shows the rule it came from, the content group(s) and language(s) it applies to, and its status.

##### Turning a Check On or Off

AI Checks are not toggled directly. Manage a check through its rule on the Rules tab. Disabling the rule disables its check.

##### Omitted Checks

Not every rule can become a check. When Phrase cannot create an AI Check from a rule, it skips the check and shows the reason, so it is always clear why a guideline is not verified automatically.

Omitted checks appear with an Omitted badge and the reason for omission. They stay disabled and do not run during quality evaluation.

#### AI Checks, Rules, and Quality Profiles

These represent three views of one model:

- **Rules** define how content should be written. AI capabilities apply rules during translation, and rules serve as the source of truth for language guidelines.
- **AI checks** are the checkable form of rules applied during quality evaluation.
- A **quality profile** is the full set of AI checks for one content group. It matches a content group one-to-one, and Phrase assigns it automatically.

Adding, editing, enabling, disabling, or reusing the checks in a quality profile happens through the [rules](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120405788#UUID-1458f46a-d027-46d2-5b96-4f01be697fc1 "Rules") behind them.

#### Limits

- A maximum of 20 AI checks apply to one content group and language combination.

---

### MCP Server (Integrations)

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/29021792139932-MCP-Server-Integrations  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:41Z  
> Labels: edited_at

##### Available for

- All Phrase plans, currently at no additional cost

The Phrase MCP Server (Model Context Protocol Server) connects AI assistants, such as Claude, to Phrase. It brings Phrase's language intelligence into these tools, allowing assistants to work with projects, jobs, and translations using natural language. The server spans Phrase TMS, Phrase Strings, and other Phrase capabilities exposed through the API, and operates above these individual products rather than within one of them.

##### Capabilities

MCP Server brings Phrase's language intelligence into AI tools already in use, enabling content, jobs, and translations to be acted on with natural language wherever the work happens. Typical tasks include:

- Asking questions about projects and jobs.
- Troubleshooting quality issues across content without opening individual files or languages.
- Creating jobs or reassigning projects.
- Performing bulk updates instead of updating one item at a time.
- Creating, editing, and deleting Phrase TMS connectors.

##### Connection Options

MCP Server supports two connection methods.

###### Remote Connection (Recommended)

Remote connection uses sign-in with an existing Phrase account and requires no installation. This method works with AI tools that support connecting to a remote server.

Remote connection authenticates using OAuth, the only authentication protocol currently supported for this method. Custom or third-party workflow tools that require manual header-based authentication are not currently supported for remote connection. This includes bearer tokens, API keys, and machine to machine (M2M) credentials.

###### Stale Authentication in VS Code

Visual Studio Code (VS Code) caches the client ID registered with an MCP server, keyed to the server URL rather than the display name. If that client ID no longer exists on the Phrase server, VS Code continues sending the stale ID instead of triggering a new registration. This results in a 400 Bad Request error reporting an unmatched `client_id` and `redirect_uri`, rather than the expected consent screen. Removing and reinstalling the server entry, or renaming it, in `mcp.json` does not clear the cached registration, since the cache is keyed to the server URL.

To resolve this issue in VS Code 1.101 or later, follow these steps:

1. Open the Command Palette (**Ctrl**/**Cmd**+**Shift**+**P**) and run Authentication: Remove Dynamic Authentication Providers, then select the Phrase MCP server entry.
2. Open the Accounts menu, locate the account tied to the Phrase server, and open Manage Trusted MCP Servers to revoke it, or sign out of that account entry.
3. In the MCP view, or via MCP: List Servers, stop and restart the server connection, or run Developer: Reload Window.

The next tool call triggers a fresh registration and displays the consent screen.

##### Note

If these steps do not clear the stale registration, clear the stored authentication manually from the operating system (OS) credential store (macOS Keychain, Windows Credential Manager, or libsecret on Linux) and VS Code's global storage. This clears authentication for any other configured MCP servers.

###### Self-Hosted Connection (Deprecated)

Self-hosted connection runs on infrastructure managed by the team and requires Node.js and a configuration file.

##### Getting Started

Find full setup steps for both connection options, including commands for different AI tools, in the Developer Hub article [Phrase MCP Server](https://developers.phrase.com/en/mcp-server).

After connecting, find a curated list of starter prompts organized by task in the Developer Hub article [Server Starter Prompts](https://developers.phrase.com/en/mcp-server-starter-prompts).

##### Permissions

When connecting, the account holder selects the level of access granted to the assistant: view data, create data, or modify and delete data. This applies in addition to existing Phrase role permissions, not instead of them, so the assistant cannot exceed what the account already allows.

Review or revoke access at any time from Phrase profile settings, under the Consents tab.

##### Related Articles

- [API (TMS)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709706916124#UUID-e66c3c22-e3d3-ade2-9f14-e1800c773c58)

### Phrase Atlas

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/29261709329308-Phrase-Atlas  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-25T06:24:00Z

#### Available for

- Team, Professional, Business, Enterprise and Software UI/UX plans

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) for licensing questions.

Phrase Atlas is a conversational AI agent for the Phrase Language Intelligence Platform, which provides direct access to Phrase platform capabilities through natural language. Instead of navigating menus or workflows, describe what you need, and Atlas executes it.

#### How Phrase Atlas Works

Atlas operates through a chat interface. Each chat is a separate thread with its own history. Atlas reads each message, decides whether the request is a question or an action, and responds in kind.

- Conceptual questions, such as "What is a [translation memory](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709688865692#UUID-c58dd835-39ea-1a94-02d1-9f2517fd6d75)?": Atlas provides an explanation.
- How-to questions, such as "How do I [create a job](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/10825587738268#UUID-f34b2b7c-0b92-e115-cfee-f436ba5e5445)?": Atlas provides step-by-step guidance.
- Action requests, such as "[Create a project](https://support.phrase.com/document/preview/100179#UUID-5c7c7145-9ac1-95c9-4e44-bad7d3b581bf)", "Update this [job's due date](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/10825557726492#UUID-e69fba74-93f9-2b07-a90c-69d3c98a8ed6)" or "Set up a [GitHub connector](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709656771356#UUID-59be0e6b-918d-2c99-67d5-9ead10cab859)": Atlas confirms the details, then executes the action.

Atlas can combine these capabilities in ways not exposed in the user interface, enabling tasks not otherwise possible without direct API use (e.g., bulk updates across multiple projects in a single request).

#### Access

Phrase Atlas is available to

- TMS Administrators and Project Managers
- Strings Owners, Administrators, Project Managers, Designers and Developers
- Platform Owners, Administrators and those who have access to Atlas in TMS or Strings

Access applies at the organization level.

Atlas respects existing role and project permissions: available actions depend on the user's permissions.

#### Using Atlas

Atlas is accessible from the main navigation or via the Ask Atlas button in the header, in the Platform Dashboard, Phrase TMS, Phrase Strings, and Phrase Studio.

Atlas operates only within the scope of Phrase products.

##### How Chat Works

To ask a question, follow these steps:

1. Open the Phrase Atlas chat via the Ask Atlas button or from the main navigation.
2. Describe your goal in plain language.
3. Send the message.
4. Atlas proposes a plan before making any changes.
5. Confirm to proceed, or refine your request.

Start a new chat using the New chat button at any time.

##### Reading Data vs. Making Changes

Atlas executes read actions (retrieving projects, jobs, translation memories, and similar data) immediately. Atlas asks one clarifying question at a time when a request is unclear or could apply to more than one product or resource. For example, if a user asks to set up a "term base" while connected to both Phrase TMS and Phrase Strings, Atlas asks which product the request applies to before proceeding, because both products use this term for related but distinct features.

If a user repeats a request without answering a clarifying question, Atlas states the assumed default and proceeds.

Atlas gates every write action (creating, updating, or deleting a project, job, translation memory, term base, or other resource) behind explicit confirmation. Atlas presents a plan and waits for explicit confirmation before executing. A short reply such as "yes, do it" confirms the plan and triggers execution.

Prerequisites: Some write actions require a specific account role or organization-level permission. For example, creating or updating a translation memory, term base, project, or user in Phrase TMS requires the [Admin](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709753812124#UUID-c512e8a2-c3ee-7321-9b76-81f0dd72517f) or [Project Manager](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709756503196#UUID-f5990e2a-6aa9-ce27-fe52-bdad461726e6) role with the relevant user permissions. The TMS Linguist, Guest, and Submitter roles cannot perform these actions.

##### Handling Credentials

Atlas never asks for a third-party secret or credential in the chat, including an API key, token, client secret, password, or service-account key file. For an action that needs such a credential, Atlas configures the non-secret parts of the resource through the API, then directs the reader to enter the credential in the named Phrase settings page, and offers to verify the connection afterward.

##### Chat History

Chats are saved and can be revisited later. Continuing a saved chat picks up from where it left off, including prior context.

Rename a chat by clicking Rename in the More menu.

Click the name of the current chat to display previous chats in a dropdown.

Chats can be deleted from the history by clicking the recycle bin icon after clicking the chat name or from the More menu.

Atlas automatically deletes the chats from history after 6 months.

##### Interrupting a Chat

Chats can be stopped before they finish.

##### Working with Files

Atlas reads files attached to a chat message and uses their content in a request. Attach a file using the paperclip icon, then reference it in the message (for example, "Count the segments in this XLIFF file").

Atlas reads common structured formats, including .CSV, .TSV, .JSON, .XML, .XLIFF, .PDF, and .XLSX.

The file upload limit is **1 GB per file**.

Atlas also produces downloadable files from data already in the chat. To export data, ask Atlas to turn a result or table into a file. Atlas generates a downloadable file in .CSV, .XLSX, or .PDF format.

#### Providing Feedback

Atlas is an AI system and can occasionally produce inaccurate results. Atlas always requests confirmation before making changes. Review the proposed plan before proceeding.

Feedback can be submitted directly through the chat interface:

- **Thumbs up**: submit positive reinforcement when Atlas performs as expected.
- **Thumbs down**: report unexpected behavior with a brief description. All feedback is reviewed by the Phrase team.

#### Limits

- **Model:** Anthropic Sonnet 5.
- There is no limit on the chat size or the number of chats.
- There is an overall limit on the utilization of Atlas per user per month.

#### Monitoring Usage

Atlas usage is tracked per user and per organization.

User usage resets at the start of each calendar month. Users can monitor their own usage, including the reset date, on the progress bar in the Atlas chat interface. The user is notified when their Atlas usage reaches 80% and Atlas is blocked when 100% is reached.

##### Best Practices for Chats

- Start a new chat for each unrelated task. Atlas carries the history of a chat into every response. Combining unrelated tasks (e.g., term base work, then a Strings task, then a user management request) in a single chat means each new task pays the cost of the previous ones' history, even though it doesn't need it.
- Keep chats focused on one goal. A chat that stays on a single task uses meaningfully less of the monthly allowance than one that drifts across multiple topics.
- Avoid pauses mid-task where possible. Resuming a chat after a five-minute gap can cost more than continuing one that is still active, since some of the earlier context needs to be reloaded.

#### Example Use Cases

| Area | Description | Prompt |
| --- | --- | --- |
| User management | Bulk deactivating a filtered group of users | Deactivate all linguists whose name starts with 'JHA.' Show me a table with the first name, last name, masked username, masked email, and activated flag. |
| Analytics and usage reporting | A data query against project/translation-management records for a specific time frame | Give me the top 10 projects from July with the highest word count. For each, list the source and target languages, status, and project owner. |
| How-to guidance | Regional localization workflow setup | I want to tailor our marketing content, which lives in WordPress, by region, and need different translation versions for Spain and Latin America. |
| Quality benchmarking | Engine comparison against a quality benchmark | Retranslate the Atlas Demo Project with the AI Translation Agent, instead of the Machine Translation engine, and use the Atlas Demo Quality Profile to compare the results. |

### Phrase AI Features Overview

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/29488451849628-Phrase-AI-Features-Overview  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-30T06:24:42Z  
> Labels: edited_at

Phrase includes several AI-powered capabilities across the platform, spanning translation, content adaptation, and quality evaluation.

##### Note

Availability varies by feature and subscription plan. For each feature's availability, detailed configuration, and usage instructions, see the dedicated articles.

##### [AI Translation Agent](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20660272640284#UUID-d2f83c62-3868-1bfe-75ae-b32b718706c9)

A GenAI-powered agent that produces context-aware translations with built-in review and refinement. It performs context-aware translation, enforces terminology, applies translation memory-grounded consistency checks (RAG), and corrects fluency and tag or formatting issues.

##### [Phrase Language AI (Machine Translation)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709660879516#UUID-b0d64b4f-fa01-f7c3-006a-40e0ee0dedd2)

Phrase's machine translation (MT) and agentic AI translation hub. It connects Phrase capabilities to MT engines (native, ecosystem, and bring-your-own) and manages how translation requests are configured, routed, and evaluated through MT profiles and MT Glossary support.

##### [Custom AI](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683093364508#UUID-9ba4dea8-4736-21c6-248a-84c1d607158a)

An environment for building custom machine translation engine models from translation memory data, covering dataset creation, training, and deployment. This allows organizations to build models tailored to their own content and terminology.

##### [MT Optimize (Orchestrator)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/20397396468508#UUID-b0ff2f2f-60e8-95fa-7166-1cbfaa9e853a)

A generative AI post-editing step in Phrase Orchestrator. It improves existing machine translation output by refining fluency, grammar, terminology, and formality.

##### [Auto Adapt (Orchestrator)](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/18807673145372#UUID-13f11225-b03e-7da3-bc9f-1db0de86ea75)

A generative AI content transformation feature in Phrase Orchestrator. It adapts content for a target audience or tone, generates variants, and adjusts reading level.

##### [AI Checks](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/28818120438300#UUID-47d194ba-1730-421a-20d0-a0c5e17987c5 "AI Checks")

Automated checks that run during quality evaluation. Each AI check comes from a rule and returns a pass or fail verdict for the content it checks. Checks are grouped automatically by content group and language into a quality profile.

##### [Phrase Atlas](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/29261709329308#UUID-b175db1f-2cd9-04b0-1787-8eb0678f2b70 "Phrase Atlas")

Phrase Atlas is a conversational AI agent for the Phrase Language Intelligence Platform, which provides direct access to Phrase platform capabilities through natural language. Instead of navigating menus or workflows, describe what you need, and Atlas executes it.

## Custom AI

Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/sections/29975499428124-Custom-AI

### Phrase Custom AI Overview

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/30487429823004-Phrase-Custom-AI-Overview  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-22T06:23:03Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

#### Available for

- Team, Professional, Business, Enterprise and Software UI/UX plans

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) for licensing questions.

Phrase Custom AI is an intuitive environment enabling the [creation of a dataset](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683072386844#UUID-09a2b5dd-ca20-1cb8-b154-e77ed7797256 "Create a Dataset"), [training an engine model](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/10414428567708#UUID-92afd96b-eb8a-9c1d-feae-9059dd5d2a9f "MT Engine Model Training"), [cleaning the engine model](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683093410076#UUID-740ff19d-1dc3-c4c9-ce4c-e435589b7a1d "Cleaning Filters") and deployment of custom machine translation (MT). Translation memories can also be cleaned and uploaded back to TMS to improve performance.

Once a dataset is created for either [Asset Curation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14319572338332#UUID-243c987b-ecb6-1d40-f50f-0da5e40e7519 "Automated Asset Curation") (used for Translation Memory (TM) cleaning), or MT Training (used for training a machine translation engine), it cannot be reused or repurposed for the other.

Custom AI usage is governed by two main quotas, which vary based on the subscription plan:

1. Trained Models (per month)

   - A limited number of custom models can be trained per billing period.
   - Once the limit is reached for that billing period, a model can be undeployed to free space for a new one.
   - Trainings are not limited to specific datasets.
2. Deployments (total active)

   - There is a cap on the total number of active custom model deployments at any point in time.

Supported languages differ depending on the dataset type. MT Training datasets support only the [language pairs supported by Phrase NextMT](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/6638250705436-Phrase-NextMT#supported-language-pairs-in-both-directions--0-1). Asset Curation datasets, used for Translation Memory cleaning, support a broader list of languages that is not limited to Phrase NextMT language pairs.

**Core functionalities:**

- **MT Engine Training**

  Create, train, evaluate, and deploy custom machine translation engines tailored to specific domains and language pairs for improved translation quality and performance.

  - **Data cleaning**

    Turn TMs into clean, high-quality datasets, ready for MT customization within Phrase Custom AI.
  - **Engine Customization**

    Develop a domain and language pair-specific model that achieves high levels of quality.
  - **Data Insights**

    Evaluate the performance of custom engines via automated evaluation.
- **[Automated Asset Curation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14319572338332#UUID-243c987b-ecb6-1d40-f50f-0da5e40e7519 "Automated Asset Curation")**

  Automatically clean and optimize Translation Memories (TMs) while preserving crucial TM metadata and original tags. This ensures high-quality matches, maintaining accuracy and efficiency for both human translators and MT engines.

[Eingebettetes Video/Inhalt](https://www.youtube.com/embed/2Z6AL3T-zeE?feature=shared)

##### Note

Due to continuous improvements, the user interface may not be exactly the same as presented in the video.

#### Accessing Custom AI

##### Available for

- Team, Ultimate and Enterprise plans (Legacy)

Get in touch with [Sales](https://phrase.com/demo/) for licensing questions.

To access Phrase Custom AI, log in through the [Phrase Platform log in page](https://eu.phrase.com/idm-ui/signin) using the same user name / e-mail address and password currently used for Phrase TMS.

- Customers using TMS SSO:

  - If SSO enforcement is required, consider setting up [Platform level SSO](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5784117127580#UUID-e03be95d-94c2-a36f-04d5-99fa2c5c6d55).
  - If applicable, log in to Platform using username and password.
- Customers using Social logins can [request a reset](https://support.phrase.com/document/preview/7928#UUID-bddb003f-dc32-06ed-f92f-75c473c51ee7) of their password that will give them Custom AI access upon logging in.

Admins can enable Custom AI access for individual users in the Phrase Platform user dashboard.

### Datasets Page

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683072422044-Datasets-Page  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:39Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

Created datasets are presented in a table on the Datasets page. Some tables can be ordered by clicking on column headings. Clicking on the dataset name opens the Overview for the selection. Datasets can be renamed or deleted from the three dot menu ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682466307612) on the overview.

The More menu ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682450131356) can be used to open the Overview, display cleaning filters and cleaning history, downloading or deleting the dataset.

In the case a cleaning fails and has an error status, click Retry ![Retry](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682462118428)from the More menu to make another attempt.

#### Dataset Overview

The overview provides information about the cleaning with tabs for reviewing the applied filters and a history of the cleaning of the selected dataset. The distribution of cleaning filters is displayed along with all metadata.

**Tabs**:

- Overview

  Displays the overview of that dataset and the distribution of cleaning filters.
- Cleaning filters

  If only cleaned once, displays the results of the filters selected for the initial training. If more cleanings have been performed, the configuration for the last cleaning is displayed.

  To change the filters, follow these steps:

  1. From the right side of the page, click Edit filters .

     The Edit cleaning filters page opens.
  2. Click Edit on a filter that requires a change.

     The filter options open.
  3. Make required changes and click Save.

     The changes are displayed and the Reclean button is activated.
  4. Click Reclean to update the cleaning.
- Cleaning history

  A table of cleanings is presented.

##### Evaluation dataset

This is only displayed if the purpose of the dataset is MT training. The number of segments selected for custom MT model evaluation is determined by the lesser of the following two criteria:

- 10% of the total dataset.
- A fixed count of 3,000 segments.

These segments are exclusively for evaluation and are not used during the training phase of the model.

---

### Create a Dataset

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683072386844-Create-a-Dataset  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:39Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

##### Translation Memory Selection Guidelines

Phrase Custom AI leverages translation memories (TMs) to create custom machine translation (MT) models that adhere to specific terminology and style, leading to improved translation quality (and thus reduced post-editing times) for these content types when compared to generic machine translation.

The most important factor that can influence the effectiveness of the customization process is the used translation memories. These are general guidelines that can help to determine what data to use for this purpose:

- *Single domain:*

  It is best if the dataset focuses on content covering a single style and terminology. If the dataset contains a mixture of domains (e.g., both the legal terms of a website and the product descriptions) the model can fail to learn what the desired style is.
- *Unique content type:*

  The custom MT model builds on top of generic models trained on vast amounts of public data collected from the internet. If the translation memory contains data which is quite similar to the generic data used to build the generic models, there will not be much to be gained from the customisation process.
- *Data quality:*

  The model will assume that every sentence pair in the translation memory is an example of the output it will be expected to produce. The translation memory must be of good quality, ideally created from professional human translations. The data cleaning pipeline can help to filter out the most harmful parts of the dataset.
- *Expected volume:*

  For the customization to be impactful in terms of RoI, the dataset needs to be representative of the bulk of the data where MT quality will have more impact. For example, if some of the MT output is to be post-edited by human translators, to maximize the RoI the data needs to be representative of the content that will be post-edited.

Creating a dataset for [automated asset curation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/14319572338332#UUID-243c987b-ecb6-1d40-f50f-0da5e40e7519 "Automated Asset Curation") has a slightly different process.

To create a dataset for the purpose of training a custom MT engine, follow these steps:

1. From the Datasets page, click Train a custom MT engine.

   The Dataset details page opens.
2. Provide a name for the dataset.
3. The language selectors allow for various options:

   1. To create a general language dataset, select the same source and target languages in the source and target language and locale selectors.
   2. To create a locale-specific dataset, select the source and target languages from the first dropdown list then specify the source and target locales from the second dropdown list.

      Multiple target locales (i.e. different variants of the same language) to leverage more data sources can also be added.
   3. To create a dataset with multiple source and target locales, select the source and target languages from the first dropdown list, specify the source and target locales from the second dropdown list (different variants of the same target language can be added) and click on + Add more locale pairs.

   The Input data window appears.
4. Click Add translation memories.

   The Choose translation memories page opens with a search functionality ![Search](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682450205340).
5. To add a TM to the dataset, click the ![Add TM](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682450233884) icon. The TM is added to the Selected column.

   Multiple TMs can be added to a maximum of 200 TMs and a maximum of 8 million segments. A dataset should ideally contain at least 10,000 segments.

   Clicking on the TM name will present the selection on the [translation memory page](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/9386902899740#UUID-bafe9a04-4afd-220a-d2e5-3063d57808bc).

   Click the ![Remove TM](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682448184604) icon to remove the TM from the Selected column.
6. Click Save.

   The Dataset details page opens.
7. Review the details as presented and if correct, click Continue.

   The Cleaning filters page opens.
8. Apply required filters and click Create.

   The dataset is created and added to the list on the Datasets page with the initial status of Cleaning and the status of Training MT in the Created for column.

### Cleaning Filters

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/7683093410076-Cleaning-Filters  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:40Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

The most important aspects of training MT engines is not just volume, but data quality. Cleaning data is a pervasive problem and manual cleaning is laborious. Clean data leads to faster training and higher quality models.

Phrase Custom AI adapts translation memories into datasets with the help of AI-powered and rule-based cleaning filters. Default settings are provided shoud be suitable for new users.

The set of filters available include both rule-based filters and ML-based filters:

- Rule-based

  Filters that operate with clearly defined rules that are easily understandable by humans. This filter category includes Date range, Minimum character count, Sentence pair length, Length ratio, Non-translatables, Duplicates, Near-duplicates.
- ML-based

  Filters that analyze the content of the text itself to make a decision, rather than simply following a fixed set of rules. This filter category includes Misaligned source and target, and Language identification.

All filters evaluate on cleaned versions of the segments; among other things, multiple spaces are reduced to one and Phrase [tags](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709695024412#UUID-9f36c0c5-d7f2-0f81-ec55-44efc63d0050) are removed.

##### Date range

Excludes segments outside of the set dates. The end and start dates are included along with the date of last modification of a segment.

##### Misaligned source and target

This filter determines how well the segments match in terms of meaning and semantic similarity, removing the worst rated. The sentence pair alignment is measured using the [LASER](https://github.com/facebookresearch/LASER) metric.

An AI engine is used to check that the source and target text mean the same thing or how much of the same thing. The recommended setting discards the 10% worst segments while keeping the 90% best segments.

Advanced settings allows changing the alignment or can be a filter based on the raw similarity score using a number between 0 and 1 (1 meaning complete alignment). Caution is advised if using the raw similarity score as each language pair has a different distribution of scores and what is considered a good score for one language pair may be an unsatisfactory score for another.

Typically segments below 0.5 are not very good and segments close or over 1 are segments that are the same in both languages.

**Examples:**

`{"source": "Super.", "target": "Super.", "similarity": 1.05}`

`{"source": "Hello", "target": "http://wwww.sdsadsa.com", "similarity": 0.3}`

##### Minimum character and letter count

*Character count* includes all characters. This includes all letters, white spaces, and punctuation and symbols. For training purposes, it may be useful to discard segments that do not contain any letters.

*Letter count* counts only letters such as in the English alphabet, but also more complex characters with diacritics or Chinese characters. One Chinese character is counted as one letter, even if it represents more than one character. For character-based languages default values are 1, but for word-based languages default values are 4 (characters) and 3 (letters). The minimum value is 1 and the maximum value is 500.

If keeping a lot of short segments in data (for example acronyms), keep the filter values low.

**Example:**

The string `"Hello, World! 1 2 3"` has 19 characters and 10 letters.

##### Sentence pair length

This filter removes all segments that are longer than the threshold value set by users. The reason for this filter is that most NMT systems will not actually train on segments that are longer than their internal threshold.

For instance, [NextMT](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/6638250705436#UUID-19cbe6e6-24a4-d991-ec7a-0241f4611a4f)’s internal threshold is 200 tokens, which equals approximately 100 - 1,000 words. To train a custom engine on shorter sentences, set this value lower than the default.

The total character count includes all characters - letters, white spaces and punctuation- from both the source and target sentences. Take the type of language into consideration (for example Chinese and English); if the source language is not CJK-like and the target language is [CJK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJK_characters) (or the other way around), this filter will be ignored.

##### Length ratio

This filter identifies segments where length is significantly higher when comparing the source segment and the target segment. Some translations increase or decrease in length when translating from a source to a target language. Too long or too short translations may indicate low-quality training data.

If the source language is not CJK-like and the target language is (or the other way around), this filter will be ignored.[CJK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJK_characters)

Some languages are more verbose than others, so 200% is a good default. If the target language is similar to the source language, or more data needs to be filtered out, the value can be lower.

**Examples:**

One language is CJK - ratio is 1. It will not be discarded:

`{"source": "This is a sentence.", "target": "这是一个句子。", "ratio": 1}`

The German translation is of comparable length as the English source and will not be discarded:

`{"source": "This is a sentence.", "target": "Dies ist ein Satz.", "ratio": 1.1}`

The German translation is a lot longer than the English source and will be discarded:

`{"source": "This is a sentence.", "target": "Dies ist ein Satz mit zusätzlichen unnötigen Füllungen.", "ratio": 3.1}`

##### Non-translatables

Non-translatables are segments where the source and target segments are the same. Excludes all non-translatable sentence pairs where the target text remains unchanged from the source text.

##### Duplicates

Groups of segments are created that have the same source sentence. From each group, only the best segment is kept so if a segment’s source sentence is unique, it is automatically kept. Otherwise. the segment with the highest similarity score is kept.

##### Near-duplicates

When testing for near-duplicates, the (slightly cleaner version of) a source sentence is normalized; all non-letter characters (some examples: `“,?)!-`) are replaced with a space and all letters are rendered lowercase.

Using the normalized source sentence, groups of segments that have the same normalized source sentence are created. From each group, only the best segment is kept so a segment’s normalized source sentence is unique and is automatically kept. Otherwise. the segment with the highest similarity score is kept.

##### Language identification

An AI engine is used to identify the source and target language based on the sentences. A segment is only removed if the engine recognizes a (source/target) language (as an example, shorter sentences are often not enough for the engine to determine a language) and the language is different than expected.

##### QPS

The [QPS](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709672289180#UUID-054981cd-9a0e-32bf-79b7-cd6167ebf444) filter removes the lowest-quality sentence pairs in the dataset to ensure that the resulting AI models are trained on the highest-quality data available. Generally, the higher the quality of training data, the better the customized model performs.

The QPS filter can be configured in two ways:

1. Removing a specified percentage of sentence pairs with the lowest QPS scores. The recommendation is 10%.
2. Selecting a score threshold. Use the advanced settings to eliminate sentence pairs falling below an adjustable QPS threshold. The recommended starting point is 50.

These two options provide automated dataset curation to align with users’ quality objectives.

### MT Engine Model Training

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/10414428567708-MT-Engine-Model-Training  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:41Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

To train an engine, follow these steps:

1. From the MT models page, click Train MT model.

   The New MT model window opens.
2. Provide a name for the model.
3. Select a Dataset from the dropdown list.

   The source and target languages are presented along with the total number of segments.

   In order to be usable for engine training, a dataset requires a minimum of 5,000 segments but ideally, more than 10,000.
4. Click Create and train.

   The name of the model is added to the list along with the state. Training can take several hours depending on number of segments.

The More menu ![ellipses.png](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682448256796) can also be used to open the Overview.

In the case a training fails and has an error status, click Retry ![Retry](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682462285084)from the More menu to make another attempt.

#### Using a Model

Once an engine is trained, the model can be used in [Phrase Language AI](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709660879516#UUID-b0d64b4f-fa01-f7c3-006a-40e0ee0dedd2).

To use the model in Phrase Language AI, follow these steps:

1. Select Deploy to Language AI (TMS) from the ellipses menu ![ellipses.png](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682448256796) of any model in the list or click Deploy to Language AI (TMS) on the model details page.

   The Deploy to Language AI (TMS) window opens.
2. Select any specific locale combination belonging to the model language pair from the Source language and Target language dropdown lists.
3. Select which MT profiles the model should be deployed to from the dropdown list.

   Click the x beside the profile name to remove it.
4. Click Deploy.

   The model will be marked both in the model list and the details page as being in a state of Deployed along with how many profiles it is deployed to. Hover over the number of deployments to see the profile names.

The model is now visible in [Phrase NextMT](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/6638250705436#UUID-19cbe6e6-24a4-d991-ec7a-0241f4611a4f) configuration.

To use the custom model for translation, create a dedicated [MT profile](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709660879516#UUID-b0d64b4f-fa01-f7c3-006a-40e0ee0dedd2) using only the customer model and then apply that model in the [machine translation](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709692009884#UUID-5ee4d7fd-b6eb-a722-73da-3fdcb23390b0) settings of a [project](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/5709748435484#UUID-30234837-9796-c6f2-1a21-a623b67ac6cc).

If deployed models are removed or undeployed, quota is freed up. Assuming the limit hasn’t changed, freed quota can then be reused to deploy new models.

To move an already-deployed model to a different MT profile, or to free its deployment quota for reuse, remove the model from its current MT profile first. This control is not available on the Custom AI MT Models page. It is located inside the Phrase Language AI MT profile that currently holds the model.

1. On the Custom AI MT Models page, copy the Custom Model ID of the model to remove.
2. In Phrase Language AI, open the MT profile that currently holds the model.
3. Click Edit next to Phrase Custom NextMT.
4. Locate the model in the list using the Custom Model ID copied earlier.
5. Click the trash icon next to the model, then save the profile.

After removal, the Custom AI MT Models page may take a few minutes to sync the updated status. Once synced, the model status changes to Ready to deploy, and the Deploy to Language AI (TMS) button becomes enabled again. The model can then be deployed to a different MT profile without deleting or retraining it and without using additional deployment quota, since the model is only moved between profiles.

If the MT profile contains only one custom model, the trash icon next to the model is not displayed, since a profile cannot be saved with empty values. In this case, toggle off Phrase Custom NextMT in the MT profile, then save the profile. This undeploys the model in Custom AI.

#### Deleting a Model

If a model is no longer required, it can be deleted from either the More menu ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682466605212) on the MT model page or from the three-dot menu ![More Menu](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682496039068) on the specific model page.

Deleting a model will remove it from the Phrase platform and undeploy it automatically.

### Automated Evaluations

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/13051542465308-Automated-Evaluations  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:42Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

Automated evaluations are provided for every MT model. Click on a model name or the ellipsis ![ellipses.png](https://support.phrase.com/hc/article_attachments/30682462381084) in the More column to view them.

Phrase Custom AI offers rich data and advanced visual support designed to provide a deeper understanding of custom NextMT model quality:

- The Overview tab provides a summary of the evaluation results, featuring intuitive visualizations and metadata about the MT model.

  - The Performance Comparison table compares the performance of generic versus custom NextMT models across four [MT quality metrics](https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/12669609584156#UUID-8b160a09-0452-538c-a67a-6392e2a40b5c "Using MT Metrics"). The table has two main sections:

    - Baseline Performance

      Shows automated MT quality scores for Phrase NextMT and a custom NextMT model without TM leverage.
    - RAG Performance

      Shows automated MT quality scores where TM fuzzy matches are leveraged to adapt MT output.

    The Best Engine column highlights the highest-performing model for each metric.
  - The Model metadata panel provides essential information about the evaluated custom NextMT model.
- The Visualizations tab provides a graphical representation of MT evaluation results through donut charts, offering a breakdown of evaluated translation segments by quality category.

  - Select the desired MT quality metric from the dropdown menu at the top to benchmark the custom NextMT model against the generic Phrase NextMT model.
  - Hover over each category of the donut chart(s) to view the percentage and number of affected segments for that category.
- The Evaluation sample tab presents a segment sample preview from the evaluation set, displaying a list of source segments with relevant baseline and RAG performance scores.

  When a segment is selected, the right panel displays:

  - Segment-specific scores and quality level indicators for baseline and RAG performance.
  - A comparison of the translation output generated by custom and generic NextMT models against the reference translation from the dataset. Select Show differences in the engine output to highlight differences against the reference translation.

### Using MT Metrics

> Quelle: https://support.phrase.com/hc/en-us/articles/12669609584156-Using-MT-Metrics  
> Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-09-15T11:22:42Z  
> Labels: 2BTr

Automated evaluation metrics play a crucial role in assessing the quality of translations produced by machine translation systems. Unlike human evaluations, which can be subjective and time-consuming, automated metrics provide a quick, objective, and repeatable way to gauge the performance of MT systems.

Phrase Custom AI incorporates several well-established automated metrics to evaluate machine translation quality: BLEU, TER, chrf3, and COMET.

It is advised to deploy customized systems to a production environment if both of the following conditions are met:

- *BLEU* improvement of at least 5 points (absolute, e.g. 40 vs 35), or *chrf* improvement of at least 4 points.
- No significant decrease of COMET score.

In most cases, improvements of this magnitude are easily noticeable for human translators and lead to improved post-editing times.

##### Recommended Approach

In general, the absolute values of the metrics vary depending on the language pair, domain and other factors. To gauge how successful the customization process was, examine the difference between the scores of the generic and customized system.

*BLEU*, *chrf* and *TER* all measure the string overlap between the MT outputs and reference translations. By definition, a significant improvement in these scores implies less post-editing effort for translators.

*COMET* measures translation quality in a general sense. *COMET* will not necessarily increase after customization (the customized system may output translations of similar quality, the difference is whether the translations match the customer’s style, tone of voice, terminology etc.). However, a significant decrease of *COMET* may signal a problem with the customized system.

##### Available Metrics

Phrase Custom AI incorporates several well-established automated metrics to evaluate machine translation quality: *BLEU*, *TER*, *chrf3*, and *COMET*. Each of these metrics offers a unique approach to assessing translation quality, catering to different aspects of translation.

###### COMET (Cross-lingual Optimized Metric for Evaluation of Translation)

- **Overview**

  COMET is a more recent metric that employs machine learning models to evaluate translations. Unlike traditional metrics, it does not solely rely on surface-level text comparisons.
- **Working Mechanism**

  COMET uses a neural network model trained on large datasets of human judgments. It assesses translations by considering various aspects of translation quality, including fluency, adequacy, and the preservation of meaning.
- **Use Cases**

  COMET is effective in scenarios where a deeper understanding of translation quality is required. It is particularly useful for evaluating translations where contextual and semantic accuracy are more important than literal word-for-word correspondence.

###### BLEU (Bilingual Evaluation Understudy)

- **Overview**

  BLEU, one of the earliest and most widely used metrics, evaluates the quality of machine-translated text by comparing it with one or more high-quality reference translations. BLEU measures the correspondence of phrases between the machine-generated text and the reference texts, focusing on the precision of word matches.
- **Working Mechanism**

  BLEU calculates the n-gram precision for various n-gram lengths (typically 1 to 4 words) and then combines these scores using a geometric mean. It also incorporates a brevity penalty to address the issue of overly short translations.
- **Use Cases**

  BLEU is particularly effective for evaluating translations where the exact matching of phrases and word order is important. However, its reliance on exact matches can be a limitation in capturing the quality of more fluent or idiomatic translations.

###### TER (Translation Edit Rate)

- **Overview**

  TER is a metric that measures the number of edits required to change a machine-translated text into a reference translation. It is based on the edit distance concept and includes operations like insertions, deletions, and substitutions. Unlike other metrics on this list, a lower TER score signifies a better translation.
- **Working Mechanism**

  TER calculates the minimum number of edits needed to transform the machine translation into one of the reference translations. The score is then normalized by the total number of words in the reference translation.
- **Use Cases**

  TER is useful for evaluating translations where the focus is on the amount of post-editing work required. It is particularly relevant in scenarios where translations will be post-edited by humans.

###### chrf3 (Character n-gram F-score)

- **Overview**

  chrf3, or character n-gram F-score, is a metric that evaluates translations based on character-level n-grams. It considers both precision and recall, providing a balance between the two.
- **Working Mechanism**

  chrf3 calculates the F-score, a harmonic mean of precision and recall, based on the overlap of character n-grams between the machine translation and the reference text.
- **Use Cases**

  chrf3 is advantageous for languages where word segmentation is challenging or for morphologically rich languages. It is also less sensitive to word order than BLEU, making it more flexible in evaluating translations with different but acceptable phrasings.

##### Score ranges

Absolute values of the metrics vary significantly depending on the language pair, domain and other factors. It is therefore difficult to establish general guidelines for interpreting score values and users should primarily decide based on the differences between the generic and the customized system (evaluated on an identical dataset).

This table can be considered a useful starting point for interpreting the values of the individual metrics:

- Scores below the low-quality MT threshold may be indicative of serious issues and such systems should typically not be deployed without further analysis.
- Scores which exceed the threshold for high-quality MT typically indicate a very well-performing system which produces fluent and adequate translations.

| Metric | Range | Low-quality MT threshold | High-quality MT threshold |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| COMET | Typically 0 to 1 | < 0.3 | > 0.8 |
| BLEU | 0 to 100 | < 15 | > 50 |
| TER | 0 to 100, *lower is better* | > 70 | < 30 |
| chrf3 | 0 to 100 | < 20 | > 60 |
